Let's understand optimal structure. We want coefficients c_k of A^2 as flat as possible. This is equivalent to designing a nonnegative polynomial A with coefficients summing 1 and squared coefficients low. This resembles "minimum peak of self-convolution" or "Besicovitch?" Could use continuous approximation. Let a(x)≥0 on [0,1], ∫a=1. Convolution (a*a)(y)=∫ a(x)a(y-x)dx. We want minimize sup_y c(y), y∈[0,2]. Score discrete ≈2n max c (with width scaling). Continuous objective minimize 2 sup c? As n large score→2? Wait discrete score=2n max coefficient. If a(x) piecewise constant heights a_i with width 1/n, coefficient c_k ≈ (1/n)(a*a)_{cont}(k/n)? Let's derive: discrete c_k =∑ a_i a_{k-i}. If continuous f(x)=n a_i? No. Suppose a_i = f(i/n)/n, sum≈∫f. Then c_k≈(1/n^2)∫? Actually ∑ f(i/n)/n * f(j/n)/n ≈ (1/n) *? Sum over pairs with i+j=k corresponds one-dimensional diagonal, not two-dimensional, so different from continuous convolution. This is not standard continuous convolution; it's coefficient of generating function, which approximates self-convolution on a 1D integer, not area integral. Thus n factor makes score ~ sup over discrete polynomial square normalized total coefficient. If we scale a_i = b_i/n? Sum b=1? For large n, c_k ≈ (1/n) * coefficients of B(x)^2 where b_i maybe? Potential approach: Use **log-concave / M-incase**? Maybe optimal A(x) is a low-pass filter with positive coefficients approximating a Fejér kernel? But square of Fejer? Could use **probability distributions** with log-concave densities? For independent random variables X,Y with same distribution on integer lattice, the sum distribution Z=X+Y. We want its max mass minimal. This is exactly: choose a probability distribution μ on {0,...,n-1} to minimize the maximum probability of X+Y. What is the minimal peak of sum of two iid discrete variables? This is a known problem: "minimum maximum of sum distribution". Scale invariant; we can choose support length n. We want sum distribution as flat as possible. There are constructions using **abelian groups / modular distributions**: If μ is uniform on a subgroup H of Z_m of size k, then μ*μ is uniform on H (not flat over whole range; zeros elsewhere, max high). To spread over whole range, use sum of independent small random variables: If μ is product of uniform distributions over multiple dimensions projected? We can use **randomized rounding** of a flat distribution over sums? The constraint is μ*μ = ν, perfectly flat would require square root of flat polynomial. Maybe use **convex optimization**: The set of achievable c is convex cone {μ*μ: μ≥0, sum μ=1} but objective max c. Not convex in μ. However we can parametrize by angles? Since A(z)^2 coefficients are quadratic in a. For fixed max M, feasibility: exists a≥0, sum=1, ∑a_i a_{k-i}≤M for all k. This is a **quadratic feasibility**. Alternative: Use **alternating least squares / Sinkhorn** to find a given c? Suppose we target c_k =1/(2n-1)+ perturb. We want find a such that a*a≈c. This is like factorization of a positive sequence into a tensor? The convolution equation triangular: c_0=a_0^2 -> a0=√c0. c1=2a0a1 -> a1=c1/(2a0). c2=a1^2+2a0a2 -> a2=(c2-a1^2)/(2a0). If we choose c almost flat, the recursive inversion may produce negative a at some steps. We need find c in the image with nonnegative inverse. This is similar to **Polynomial square root with nonnegative coefficients**. We can optimize c (nonnegative, sum1) to minimize max c and be square of nonnegative. Maybe a simple explicit family can beat 1.5027. Let's explore multi-block step functions (piecewise constant with a few levels) and optimize parameters. The previous SOT may find near-optimal with many blocks; but we can design targeted.

Consider sequence as concatenation of K blocks of constant height. Let masses p_j, lengths l_j (sum n). The convolution coefficient at lag k is sum over block pairs (r,s) of overlap_count between block r shifted and block s times h_r h_s. For large n, the coefficients are piecewise linear (sum of trapezoids). We can choose block order and parameters to balance peaks. The two-block optimum maybe ~1.6. Multi-block could flatten by splitting large block into pieces to reduce self peaks and overlap.

This is analogous to approximating a flat convolution using a step function. Let's derive a direct optimization method for normalized a. Objective f(a)=max_k ∑a_i a_{k-i}. Equivalent to minimize log-sum-exp: F(a)=β log ∑ exp(β c_k/ ?). But c_k quadratic. Gradient ∂c_k/∂a_j = ∑_i δ_{i+j=k} a_i + ∑_i δ_{j+i=k} a_i? Since c_k=∑_{i=0}^k a_i a_{k-i} with a out of range zero. Derivative: ∂c_k/∂a_j = a_{k-j} + a_{k-j} unless k-2j=0? More generally c_k=∑_i a_i a_{k-i}. If k even and i=k/2, term a_{k/2}^2 derivative 2a_{k/2}; but formula a_{k-j} counted twice? For j, terms i=j gives a_j a_{k-j}; i=k-j gives a_{k-j}a_j. If j≠k-j same term counted twice -> derivative 2a_{k-j}; if j=k/2, term is same i? In sum over i=0..2n-2? Actually with a_i defined length n, c_k = ∑_{i:0≤i<n,0≤k-i<n} a_i a_{k-i}. If j equals k-j (k even, j=k/2), the term i=j appears once in sum, derivative 2a_j (derivative of a_j^2). The formula 2a_{k-j} works? If k-j=j, gives 2a_j. If different, terms i=j and i=k-j both exist, each derivative a_{k-j} and a_j? For term a_j a_{k-j}, derivative w.r.t a_j = a_{k-j}; term a_{k-j} a_j derivative = a_j; total 2a_{k-j}=2a_j? not equal if a_j≠a_{k-j}. Maybe we can replace LP with a **dual subgradient** method: The optimum of LP max ∑g subject to A g≤M can be found via dual min M∑λ s.t. A^Tλ≥1, λ≥0, where λ_k are weights on convolution lags. Since A^Tλ_j=∑_k λ_k f_{k-j}. The dual has 2n variables and n constraints. Could use projected subgradient for weights λ to find a distribution over lags that defines gradient. Indeed, the SOT smooth_step with p_k=exp((c-M)/T) approximates dual weights. The optimal dual weights correspond to active lags maximizing f*g? Hmm. The gradient of max c at a is 2 A^T λ, where λ is any feasible dual of max? This is exactly subgradient. The smooth p weights are a stochastic approximation to dual extreme points. We can improve by solving the dual (or approximating) to get a better subgradient.

### Theoretical insights: low peak sum distributions

We are choosing μ on {0,...,n-1} to minimize max of μ*μ. This is equivalent to forming random variable S=X+Y with X,Y iid. We want S as uniform as possible over {0,...,2n-2}. If we could choose X such that X+X is uniform, that's impossible due to support? But we can approximate using **mixtures of binomials**: Let X = sum of independent digits, each digit uniform over intervals, and map to [0,n). For example, choose base representation: X = ∑_{r=1}^d U_r B^r with U_r uniform digits. The sum of two such variables in base B with carries. Without carries (mod B^d), the sum distribution is product of triangular digits, not uniform. With modulo B^d (cyclic group), if X is uniform on subgroup? Hmm.

What if choose X as a **random variable with independent high and low parts** such that sum distribution factorial? For B=2, if X has bits independent fair, then X is uniform on [0,2^d-1]. Sum distribution triangular-ish? Actually uniform over 0..2^d-1, not sum of independent Bernoulli? Uniform bits are independent, X uniform. Sum of two independent uniform [0,2^d-1] is symmetric with many lags, max ~1/2^d, score 2. Not good.

We need distribution whose convolution is flatter than uniform sum. That means X should not be uniform over an interval; it should be a "Sidon-like" dense set? But dense set yields random peak. Maybe use a distribution that is **low autocorrelation in sum**, akin to "Barker" for real probabilities.

Could use **Hadamard / Kac ring**: Let X be sum of ±1 increments mapped? For example, take a random walk on integers with steps ±1 for d steps, starting at d/2, support length 2d+1. The distribution is binomial, triangular peak (Central limit) max ~1/√d, support width ~d, but length n~d? Score? μ*μ (sum of two random walks) is convolution of binomials, width ~2d, peak ~1/√d. n~d, max ~1/√d, score ~2√d, bad for large d.

We want support length n ~ number of possible values, max probability ~1/n. That requires distribution close to uniform on a set that is **sum-flat**. Perfect difference sets over linear group? Dense Sidon sets with all sums equally represented are like **graceful permutations**? If S is a set of size m≈n (dense), average representation ~m^2/(2n)≈n/2, huge; need each sum represented ~n/2, i.e., the addition table is a Fourier/B_2[g] sequence with g≈n/2. This is like a **Costas array**? For 0/1 dense, c_k is the number of pairs; need all sums have about half of n pairs. This is equivalent to a binary sequence with **low autocorrelation?** But since cross with reverse, if sequence is 0/1 dense, c_k =∑ a_i a_{k-i}. This is not autocorrelation (no shift difference); it's the coefficient of square. For dense 0/1, c_k near average n/2. Is there a binary sequence with all square coefficients balanced? This is related to **balanced sequences** / **Fejér kernel**? For binary, c_k = number of 1s at i and k-i. If sequence is a "perfect complementary sequence" of order 2? ### Use coordinate/block local improvement with direct max formula

Objective global:
```
def _objective_global(x):
    ctx = _OBJ_CTX
    w = x
    # softmax
    w_shift = w - np.max(w)
    e = np.exp(w_shift)
    a = e / e.sum()
    c = fft_conv(a)
    M = c.max()
    # logsumexp with temperature
    T = ctx['T']
    if T <=0: return M
    s = c - c.max()
    # to include scale: logsumexp(c/T) = c.max()/T + T*log(sum exp(s/T))
    val = c.max()/T + T*np.log(np.sum(np.exp(s/T)))
    return val
```

def _gradient_global(x):
    ctx = _OBJ_CTX
    w=...

    c=fft_conv(a)
    M=int c.max()

    # grad_w = a*(r - sum(a*r)) * 2/T? Scale can drop 2/T.
    mean_wr = np.dot(a, r)
    grad = a*(r - mean_wr)
    # For stability, maybe subtract small? Sum grad w = ∑a grad =? = a*(r-mean); sum = ∑a*r - mean*∑a =0.

```

### Local random perturbation

Use multiplicative noise with block-scale. Previous had placeholder bug? It had `h[start:start+width] *= np.exp(float(sigma) * scale if False else 0.0)` weird. We'll implement clean:
```
def random_perturb(a, rng, sigma):
    a=a.copy()
    if a.size>1:
       a *= np.exp(rng.normal(0, sigma, size=n))
       # block perturb
       width = rng.integers(max(1,n//100), max(2,n//10)+1)
       start = rng.integers(0, max(1,n-width+1))
       a[start:start+width] *= np.exp(rng.normal(0, sigma*2, size=width))
    return normalize_nonneg(a)
```
Use only when stuck.

### Continuous density via Fourier and water-filling

We can think of choosing f such that F(θ)^2? Our discrete c coefficients are not Fourier autocorrelation but cross-correlation with reverse. Let b_i=a_{n-1-i}. Then c = b*a, coefficients of B(z)A(z). The sum coefficients =1. We want all coefficients low. In Fourier on length L≥2n-1, coefficients of B A. If B≈A? Not.

Maybe choose A(z) such that A(z)B(z)≈constant (flat polynomial). Since B is reverse of A. This means A(z) is approximately a **self-reciprocal** polynomial with constant product? If A(z)B(z) flat, then A(z)A(z^{-1}) z^{n-1} flat. This is magnitude squared on unit circle. We want |A(e^{iθ})|^2 flat, and A has nonnegative coefficients. That is a **flat magnitude filter** with nonnegative impulse response! This is exactly designing a nonnegative FIR filter with flat power response. But wait B(z)=z^{n-1} A(z^{-1}); product A(z)B(z) = z^{n-1} A(z)A(z^{-1}). Its coefficients are convolution a*a (reverse), i.e., c. So yes! The coefficients c are the autocorrelation of the impulse response a (aperiodic). The polynomial A(z)B(z) has coefficients c. We want its coefficients as flat as possible. On the unit circle, A(z)B(z) = |A(e^{iθ})|^2 (up to phase). If c coefficients are flat, then |A(e^{iθ})|^2 is nearly constant. Thus the problem is: find a nonnegative FIR filter of length n with as flat frequency response magnitude as possible, given coefficient sum normalized. This is a known problem: **nonnegative time-domain impulse response with constant magnitude response** (all-pass? high-pass?). A nonnegative window with flat magnitude? The ideal prolate? This is related to **lowpass windowing**: A nonnegative kernel (like Haar, Gaussian) whose Fourier transform has low peak. Our objective is time-domain autocorrelation peak, equivalent to frequency-domain? Parseval: max coefficient of autocorrelation vs average? The average coefficient = (sum a)^2/(2n). Flat autocorrelation means constant |A|^2. For nonnegative a, can we approximate a constant-magnitude filter? Yes, **Kaiser-Bessel?** Nonnegative filter with flat magnitude over stopband? Hmm.

If |A|^2 constant over 2n-1 Fourier samples (periodized), the a sequence would be inverse DFT of constant (A? Not square root) with nonnegative real? If |A|^2 flat, A has constant magnitude, a kernel? For length n, if |A|^2 flat, then c cyclic is flat C. Then linear c near flat except edges. We need nonnegative a. What filters have nonnegative impulse and flat magnitude? The **Fejér kernel** is square of Dirichlet, not flat. The square root of a flat autocorrelation with nonnegative coefficients might be approximated by a **prolate spheroidal wave** truncated? Prolate tapers have nonnegative data spaces but not impulse.

This frequency view suggests using **window design** algorithms: Design nonnegative FIR filter with prescribed autocorrelation flat. This is like **Sidon filters**, **Golay complementary**? There are "low autocorrelation sequences with real nonnegative amplitudes" via **Ali-Ziv-Zakai**? Could use iterative **Brandwood-Patnaik**? The SOT is one.

### Time allocation and practical algorithm

Given constraints, I think implementing a robust hybrid local search with L-BFGS, FFT subgradient, LP directions, and multiple initializations will likely beat 1.514. But need ensure it reaches <1.5027. Let's think if L-BFGS from provided 1.514 can squeeze 0.01. Provided sequence is a local optimum of SOT; L-BFGS may find a different local optimum with lower score if we use separate objective (logsumexp vs max) and softmax. It might find 1.50. We can also run from different initializations to find better basin.

Can we use a **genetic/differential evolution** for many short trajectories? With 1800s, we can run e.g. 30 trajectories of 30s each at n=512 using gradient/LP. Need diverse initializations: previous best, upsampled, block, smooth, random. We can maintain global best and use it to seed later.

Potential issue: Previous best may already be very good; L-BFGS might stagnate at same. Need perturb and use higher temperature. Temperature T can explore broad. We can run a **simulated annealing** style: use logsumexp with larger T (optimizes l1-like) to escape, then cool.

### Let's design a self-contained optimizer

Pseudo:
```
deadline = time.monotonic() + budget - 60
state = {'score':inf,'seq':None}
best_candidates = [] # list (score, seq)
add_candidate(seq): normalize, score_fast, if finite and < state score + maybe store top 10, update state; maybe official periodically.

# initial candidates
provided = load_sequence('height_sequence_1') -> resize? add.
add simple two_block_best for N=512,1024.
add smooth initializations.

# For several tasks:
tasks = [(N, seq, current, time, method_flags)]
for each reserve if enough time:
   optimize_trajectory(seq, time_limit, state, cfg)
```

`optimize_trajectory`:
- n = len(a). Set best local.
- Choose methods adaptively:
  - if n<=1024: run L-BFGS a few times (maxiter 150) with T values.
  - then loop until time: alternating:
    1. gradient step (multiplicative) with temp varying.
    2. if time and n<=768: LP tangent/SOT every few iterations.
    3. if stuck: random perturbation.
- Return best.

Need avoid too much code but okay.

#### L-BFGS details within trajectory

`run_lbfbs(a, time_sub, state)`:
- n; if n>2048 maybe skip.
- initial M = max conv. T_values relative to M: [0.25,0.10,0.04,0.015,0.005] maybe times M. De-risk overflow: T = max(min_T, frac*M). min_T maybe 1e-9.
- For each T, set global context {n,T,state}. w = np.log(a+1e-14); w -= w.mean().
- Check time; call minimize with maxiter=100/200. `minimize` returns res. We update state in objective. After call, convert best? The global state seq may have been updated from context; good.
- But objective uses `fft_conv`; if state update uses normalized a. We'll implement in `_objective_global` to update state? It might update many times and call print? Avoid prints too frequent. It can update if score < state score - 1e-12.

Potential issue: `minimize` may call objective many times and state updates from a temporary w that is not final; if we store seq, good. But if later w overflows? We keep.

- To avoid negative official due to zeros, normalize with floor? `normalize_nonneg` clips zeros; okay.

#### Gradient loop

```
def gradient_loop(a, time_limit, state, use_lp=True, use_lbfbs=True):
   a=normalize
   c=fftconv(a); M=c.max(); score=2nM
   update
   temp_frac=0.2
   no_improve=0; it=0
   last_lp = 0; last_lbfbs=time
   while time<limit:
      it++
      c=fftconv(a); M=c.max(); cur=2nM
      if cur<best: update; no_improve=0; temp_frac=max(0.02,temp*0.97)
      else no_improve++
      # if no_improve>20, perturb, temp up
      # choose step:
      if it%5==0 and n<=768 and time_since_lp > some and linprog:
          try first_order direction; if improve update; continue
      # multiplicative gradient step
      d = r - dot(a,r) (natural)
      step = natural_step(a,d)
      if no better and additive: ...
      if no move: random perturb
```

Need compute `r` once: `r = cross(p,a)`. For p = softmax((c-M)/T). T=temp_frac*M. For natural gradient, `d = a*(r - np.dot(a,r))`? But the step in w coordinates; applying a = exp(w - eta d): Since current a=exp(w) normalized, new a = normalize(a * exp(-eta d)). If d = r - mean_a? Let's use direct w_d = r - mean_weighted? From logsumexp scaled objective, grad_w = a*(r - mean_wr). To step w <- w - eta grad_w, a <- a * exp(-eta a*(r-mean_wr)). This includes factor a, making step small where a small. Maybe too conservative. Alternatively, natural gradient for log-sum objective often is a*(∇a - a E), good. We can use it. For additive, d_add = r - r.mean().

In line search, scale by max(abs(d)). For natural, d has scale a*r ~ (1/n)*(1/n) maybe 1/n^2, while additive r scale 1/n. Use alphas larger. We can just implement multiplicative with `exponent = -alpha * d / d_norm` where d_norm=max(abs(d)). For small a, log issues if a=0; add floor.

Maybe simpler: Use additive gradient because previous worked. We can also use L-BFGS for stronger optimization. Keep both.

#### LP tangent direction line search

After solving g sum1, correct g=normalize_nonneg(g). Then for t in (0.1,0.25,0.5,0.75,1.0): h=normalize((1-t)a+t g); score; choose best. Update if better. This is A; we should handle exceptions/time.

SOT direction:
```
g = solve max sum g s.t. conv(a,g)≤M, g≥0
if sum_g >1: g/=sum_g; line search
```
This LP objective max sum can be solved via linprog with c=-ones. We can maybe skip first-order and use SOT only; less complex. But first-order with gamma may find better directions. Include both? Might be too slow. We can implement one: SOT is known. However SOT uses RHS M independent of c; first-order could be more effective. We can implement active LP with cutting plane for first-order; if solve fails, fallback SOT.

Given time, code length manageable.

### Use continuous optimal control via control of convolution at peaks

We can implement a **control algorithm** on the continuous interval: We want keep C(t) below T. This is similar to water-filling. At t where C near max, reduce density where it contributes. The gradient is `r_j = ∑ p_k a_{k-j}`; moving density away from positions that contribute to peak lags. This is what SOT does. Good.

We can implement a **multiobjective L-BFGS cooling**:
```
def run_lbfbs(a, deadline, state):
    M = score_max(a)
    for frac in (0.5,0.2,0.1,0.05,0.02,0.01):
       if time > deadline - 20: break
       T=frac*M
       ctx = {'n':n,'T':T,'state':state}
       global _CTX; _CTX=ctx
       w = np.log(a+1e-15); w -= w.mean()
       maxiter = 100 if n>512 else 200
       minimize(fun=_obj_global, jac=_grad_global, x0=w, method='L-BFGS-B', options={'maxiter':maxiter, 'ftol':1e-12, 'gtol':1e-6, 'maxls':16, 'disp':False})
       # after each, convert res.x to a, update state; continue from updated a (normalize state seq)
       a = state['seq'] if state score < local? else res.x a
```
Need be careful state may update from another sequence (global contradiction)? In single-thread, state updated by objective calls with same sequence family. We can update local `a_best` separately. But global state can be used. Use local `local_best_score` and if objective updates global, also local? We can't easily retrieve. In objective global we have ctx['local_score'] list mutable? We can use a list `ctx['best_score']=[initial_score]` and update. Since lists mutable. Then after minimize, use `ctx['best_w']`? We can store best `w` in ctx when score improves. In objective, compute `a` and score; if score < ctx['best_score'][0], set `ctx['best_score'][0]=score`, `ctx['best_w']=w.copy()`, and call `update_global(state,a,score)`. Then after minimize, return best_w converted. This avoids relying on state.

Define ctx as dict with 'best_score': [inf], 'best_w': None. But storing best_w every improvement in objective may copy 512 array many times; okay.

Global functions:
```
_CTX = None
def _softmax(a_w): ... top-level
def _objective_global(x):
    ctx = _CTX
    w=x
    w_shift = w - np.max(w)
    e = np.exp(w_shift)
    a = e / e.sum()
    c = fft_conv(a)
    M = float(c.max())
    T = float(ctx['T'])
    # score
    score = 2.0 * a.size * M
    if score < ctx['best_score'][0] - 1e-15:
         ctx['best_score'][0]=score
         ctx['best_w']=w.copy()
         update_global(ctx['state'], a, score)
    # objective
    s = c - M
    # if T <= 0: return M
    val = 0.5*M + 0.5*T*np.log(np.sum(np.exp(s/T)))
    return float(val)
```
But if T very small and s/T < -700, exp underflow okay (0). If s/T overflows? s≤0, no. If T=0 avoid.
Gradient:
```
def _gradient_global(x):
   ctx=_CTX; w=x; a=softmax; c=fft_conv; M=c.max(); T=ctx['T']
   s=c-M; p=np.exp(s/T); p_sum=p.sum(); if p_sum<=0: p=zeros, p[argmax]=1 else p/=p_sum
   r = cross_power(p,a)
   # For objective derivative of F_scaled = 0.5 logsumexp? Wait F=0.5M +0.5T log S. Does derivative equal 0.5 r? Let's recalc carefully.
```
We need consistency. Let's derive exact for F = 0.5 * M + 0.5 * T * log ∑ exp((c_k - M)/T). But M = max(c) is a function of a, so derivative includes subgradient of M, not equal r. We earlier used F_scaled = 0.5*T* logsumexp(c/T), which equals 0.5*M + 0.5*T* log ∑ exp((c-M)/T). This is exactly F = (T/2) logsumexp(c/T). Its derivative does not include derivative of M as separate because logsumexp formula derivative = (1/T)∑p∂c. The expression with M is just algebra; derivative of the max term cancels? Let's check: L(c)=logsumexp(c/T)=M/T + log∑exp((c-M)/T). Derivative = p/T, where p=softmax(c/T). The derivative of M/T plus log term's M dependence yields same. So gradient of F_scaled = (T/2)*∇L = (T/2)*(1/T)∑p∂c =0.5∑p∂c = r (since ∂c=2r). Great! So grad_w = a*(r - a·r). Consistent. Good.
Thus use objective F = T/2 * logsumexp(c/T) computed as 0.5*M + 0.5*T*logS. Gradient = a*(r - dot(a,r)). Nice.

When T is small, objective ≈0.5M + small; gradient subgrad of max scaled by a. Fine.

`cross_power(p,a)` returns length n: r[j] = ∑_i a_i p_{i+j}. Need implement with FFT. Previous `shift_correlation(p, f)` returns r[j] = ∑ f[i] p[i+j], yes. We'll use `cross_correlate(p,a)`.

FFT cross implementation:
Given p length 2n-1, a length n. Need r_j = ∑_{i=0}^{n-1} a_i p_{i+j}. We can compute via convolution: r = ifft(fft(a) * conj(fft(p?)). Let's stick to previous method (works):
```
need = n + len(p) - 1
size=1; while size<need: size*=2
fa=np.zeros(size); fa[:n]=a
pb=np.zeros(size); pb[:len(p)] = p[::-1]
out = irfft(rfft(fa)*rfft(pb), size)
idx = (len(p)-1) - np.arange(n)
return out[idx]
```
Test: p length m. conv fa with reversed p: out[t] = ∑_i a_i p_rev[t-i] = ∑_i a_i p[m-1-(t-i)] = ∑_i a_i p[m-1 - t + i]. We want i+j? r_j =∑_i a_i p[i+j]. Set m-1 - t + i = i+j -> t=m-1-j. So out[m-1-j] = r_j. idx = m-1 - arange(n). Good.
Need ensure length n <= m? p length 2n-1, so idx positive for j<n: m-1-(n-1)=n-1 ≥0. Good.
Use `np.fft.rfft` real. Return real.

For convolution `fft_conv(a)`: size power2 >=2n-1. `fa[:n]=a`; `out=irfft(rfft(fa)**2, size)` returns length size; first 2n-1. Clip small negative? Since nonnegative, but rounding. Use `out[:2n-1]`. Max maybe slightly over; okay. To avoid imaginary, `np.real`.

def build_active_matrix(f, rows): ...
def solve_lp_sot(f,M,rows):
    A = build_active_matrix(f, rows)
    A=A/M
    res=linprog(-ones, A_ub=A, b_ub=ones, bounds=(0,None), method='highs')
    if res.status==0: g=res.x; g=clip; if sum>1: return g/sum
```

def lp_tangent_direction(a,c,M, max_rows=256, tol=1e-8):

 rows = choose_active_rows(c, max_rows)
 A = 2*build_active_matrix(a, rows)  # row A_kg = ∑ 2 a_{k-j} g_j
 # variable vector [g, gamma]
 c_obj = [0]*n+[1]
 A_ub = hstack(A, -ones) b=0
 A_eq = [ones,n,0]
 b_eq=1
 bounds [(0,None)]*n + [(-M*10, None)]
 res=linprog(c_obj, A_ub, b_ub, A_eq,b_eq, bounds=bounds, method='highs')
 if res.status==0:
    gamma=res[-1]
    g=res[:-1]
    # verify all constraints: L=2*conv(a,g)-c; maxL=max(L)
    # if maxL > gamma+1e-5 and rows not all, maybe add row and resolve once
    if maxL > gamma + 1e-6 and len(rows)<2n-1:
        add row argmax L; re-solve with rows+new
    if gamma < M - 1e-6: return g
```

### L-BFGS run function

```
def run_lbfbs(a, sub_time, state):
    n=a.size
    if minimize is None or n>2048: return a
    deadline = time.monotonic()+sub_time
    best=a.copy(); best_score=score_fast(a); update_global
    M=score_max(a)
    fracs=(0.35,0.15,0.07,0.03,0.012,0.005)
    for frac in fracs:
       if time.monotonic() > deadline - 15: break
       T=max(1e-10, frac*best_score/(2*n)) ? Use current M.
       ctx = {'T':T, 'state':state, 'best_score':[best_score], 'best_w':None}
       global _CTX; _CTX=ctx
       w=np.log(best+1e-15); w -= w.mean()
       maxiter = 80 if n>1024 else 150
       try:
          res = minimize(_objective_global, w, jac=_gradient_global, method='L-BFGS-B', options={'maxiter':maxiter, 'ftol':1e-12, 'gtol':1e-7, 'maxls':20, 'disp':False})
       except Exception:
          res=None
       # convert best_w or res.x
       w_best = ctx['best_w'] if ctx['best_w'] is not None else (res.x if res is not None else w)
       a_new = softmax(w_best)
       sc = score_fast(a_new)
       if sc < best_score: best=a_new; best_score=sc
       # update global
       update_global(state,best,best_score)
    return best
```
Need set global `_CTX` before calling. In Python, to modify global in function, use `global _CTX`. Top-level okay.

`softmax` function stable:
```
def softmax_from_log(w):
    w = w - np.max(w)
    e=np.exp(w); s=e.sum(); return e/s
```

Objective should not update state if sequence length different? ctx state. It calls `update_global(ctx['state'], a, score)`. That function may call `score_exact`? Avoid exact in objective. Use fast.

`update_global` should be side-effect; no prints every tiny improvement. Print if score < state score - 1e-5 and less than some count? Use `print` with flush maybe. Limit prints using state['prints']? Simpler: print if improvement >1e-6 and (not finite old or score < old - 1e-4). Objective can print a lot if L-BFGS finds improvements; condition >1e-4 maybe ends few.

Need avoid global state being updated by objective with a sequence that is not yet normalized? It is normalized via softmax.

### Smoothing step function

```
def smooth_step_from_frac(a,c,M,temp_frac,cur_score):
    T=temp_frac*M
    s=(c-M)/T; clip s -50,0; p=exp(s); if sum<=0: p[argmax]=1 else p/=sum
    r=cross_pow(p,a)
    best=a; best_cur=cur_score
    # additive
    d=r-r.mean(); scale=max(abs(d),1e-300)
    for alpha in (0.02,0.05,0.1,0.25,0.5):
       h=normalize_nonneg(a - alpha*scale*d)
       sc=score_fast(h); if sc<best_cur: ...
    # natural multiplicative
    dnat=a*(r - np.dot(a,r)); scale=max(abs(dnat),1e-300)
    for alpha in (0.1,0.3,1.0,3.0):
       h=normalize_nonneg(a * np.exp(-alpha*scale*dnat))
       sc=score_fast(h); ...
    return best if best_cur<cur else None
```
But computing score for each alpha (FFT) many; okay.

#### Function: `fft_conv`

```
def fft_conv(a):
    n = a.size
    if n == 0: return np.array([], dtype=float)
    m = 2*n - 1
    size = 1
    while size < m:
        size <<= 1
    fa = np.zeros(size, dtype=np.float64)
    fa[:n] = a
    fa = np.fft.rfft(fa)
    out = np.fft.irfft(fa * fa, size)
    out = out[:m]
    # clean tiny negative
    out[out < 0] = 0.0? But if true nonnegative, setting negative to 0 can artificially reduce max? Out negatives only rounding near zero; max unaffected. Set small negative to 0.
    return out
```
Should we use `fa*np.conj(fa)`? For real, `fa*fa` not correct: rfft returns complex; square of complex not magnitude squared; need `fa * np.conj(fa)`? Wait convolution in frequency: FFT(f)*FFT(f), not magnitude. For linear convolution (autoproduct polynomial), it's product of DFTs, not pointwise magnitude (that would circular autocorrelation). For real f, rfft complex, product `fa*fa` is correct (not conjugate). Since `irfft` expects Hermitian; product of a real FFT with itself is not necessarily Hermitian? Convolution of a with itself should be real; in frequency, F(k)^2, which is Hermitian because F(-k)=conj(F(k)), so F(-k)^2=conj(F(k))^2=conj(F(k)^2). rfft stores positive; multiplying by itself is fine. `irfft(fa*fa)`. Good.

Cross power: use `rfft(fa) * rfft(pb)` where pb reversed as real; no conjugate for convolution. Good.

#### `cross_pow`

```
def cross_pow(p, a):
    n=a.size; m=p.size; need=n+m-1; size power2; fa[:n]=a; pb[:m]=p[::-1]; out=irfft(rfft(fa)*rfft(pb)); idx=m-1-np.arange(n); return out[idx]
```
Need p length 2n-1? If p length not, okay. But p may have length >n. idx first maybe m-1, last m-n. nonnegative if m>=n. For p length 2n-1, yes. Clip small negative? r should be nonnegative? p,a≥0 so yes; set `out[idx]>0` small negative 0.

#### `score_fast`

```
def score_fast(a):
   a=normalize_nonneg(a)
   n=a.size
   if n<1 or sum<... return inf
   c=fft_conv(a)
   M=float(c.max())
   return 2.0*n*M
```
`normalize_nonneg` already used; don't double inside objective maybe.

For softmax objective, a already normalized nonnegative; use `fast_score_from_a(a)` without normalize? Use internal with `s=a.sum()`? If due to rounding sum not 1, score formula uses actual sum? We optimize normalized sum exactly 1. Use `score = 2n max(c) / (sum^2)`. For normalized softmax sum1, same. To be safe, compute with sum. In objective, after softmax sum1. We can use `2*n*M`. def make_initial(N, kind, rng, best_seq, provided_seq):
   N=int(N)
   if kind==-1 and best_seq is not None: return resize_to_length(best_seq,N)
   if kind==-2 and provided_seq is not None: return resize_to_length(provided_seq,N)
   x=np.linspace(0,1,N)
   if kind==0: gaussian
   elif kind==1: exp skew
   elif kind==2: beta
   elif kind==3: two-block optimized random length/mass
   elif kind==4: random blocks K
   elif kind==5: lowpass noise
   elif kind==6: decreasing exponential / rational
   elif kind==7: three-block
   else random

#### LP implementation details

Choose active rows:
```
def choose_rows(c, max_rows):
    m=c.size
    if max_rows >= m: return np.arange(m)
    k=int(min(max_rows,m))
    return np.argpartition(c, -k)[-k:]
```
If c has NaN? no.

Build active matrix:
```
def active_conv_matrix(a, rows, scale=1.0):
    n=a.size; A=np.zeros((len(rows),n), float)
    for idx,k in enumerate(rows):
        k=int(k)
        lo=max(0, k-n+1); hi=min(n, k+1)
        if hi>lo:
            j= np.arange(lo,hi)
            A[idx, lo:hi] = scale * a[k - j]
    return A
```
For SOT constraints: A g ≤ M -> divide by M. For tangent: A=2*matrix, no divide? We can keep M in RHS? For tangent, constraints A g - gamma ≤0, A O(1) values ~2/n? A elements a ~0.002, fine.

`solve_lp_sot`:
```
def solve_lp_sot(a, M, rows):
    if linprog is None or M<=0: return None
    A=active_conv_matrix(a, rows,1.0)
    if not np.any(A): return None
    A=A/float(M)
    res=linprog(-np.ones(len(a)), A_ub=A, b_ub=np.ones(A.shape[0]), bounds=(0.0,None), method='highs')
    if res.status==0 and res.x is not None:
       g=np.clip(res.x,0,None); S=g.sum()
       if S>1+1e-6: return g/S
    return None
```

`solve_lp_tangent`:
```
def solve_lp_tangent(a, c, M, rows=None, max_rows=256, max_iterations=2):
    if linprog is None: return None
    n=a.size
    if rows is None: rows=choose_rows(c, min(2*n-1, max_rows))
    best_gamma=inf; best_g=None
    current_rows=rows.copy()
    for _ in range(max_iterations):
       A=active_conv_matrix(a, current_rows, 2.0)
       R=A.shape[0]
       c_obj=np.zeros(n+1); c_obj[-1]=1.0
       A_ub=np.hstack((A, -np.ones((R,1))))
       b_ub=np.zeros(R)
       A_eq=np.zeros((1,n+1)); A_eq[0,:n]=1.0
       b_eq=np.array([1.0])
       bounds=[(0.0,None)]*n + [(-10.0*M, None)] if M>0 else [(-1,None)]
       # HiGHS may be sensitive to bound -inf; use lower -1.0 maybe
       res=linprog(c_obj, A_ub=A_ub, b_ub=b_ub, A_eq=A_eq, b_eq=b_eq, bounds=bounds, method='highs')
       if res is None or res.status !=0: break
       gamma=float(res.x[-1]); g=np.clip(res.x[:n],0,None); S=g.sum()
       if S <=0: break
       g = g/S? The equality sum g=1 should hold; normalize for safety.
       # Check all constraints
       L = 2.0 * fft_conv_cross(a,g) - c # cross convolution a*g? Need c from a*a. Use np.convolve(a,g) maybe.
       maxL=float(np.max(L)); rmax=int(np.argmax(L))
       if gamma < best_gamma: best_gamma=gamma; best_g=g.copy()
       if maxL > gamma + 1e-7 and len(current_rows) < 2*n-1:
           if rmax not in current_rows: current_rows=np.unique(np.concatenate([current_rows,[rmax]]))
           else: break
       else:
           break
    if best_g is not None and best_gamma < M - 1e-8:
        return normalize_nonneg(best_g), best_gamma
    return None
```
Need `fft_conv_cross(a,g)`: can use `np.convolve(a,g)` for n<=2048 exact; or FFT. Let's implement `cross_conv(a,b)` similar to convolution without square, using FFT: size>=2n-1; `out=irfft(rfft(a)*rfft(b))[:2n-1]`. Since a,b nonnegative, okay. Use for LP verification. If n=2048, direct maybe 4M; use FFT.

`active_conv_matrix` for tangent uses `a[k-j]`; for cross convolution a*g constraints linear ∑ a_i g_{k-i}, same. Good.

```

def update_global(state, seq, score):
    if not np.isfinite(score): return
    seq = np.asarray(seq,float)
    if seq.size==0: return
    seq = normalize_nonneg(seq)
    if score >= state['score'] + 1e-12: return
    old=state['score']
    state['score']=float(score)
    state['seq']=seq.copy()
    top=state.get('top')
    if top is None:
       top=[]; state['top']=top
    # Only keep candidates significantly better or top not full
    if len(top)<12 or score < (top[-1][0] if top else inf) or score < state['seq'].size? no
       top.append((float(score), seq.copy()))
       top.sort(key=lambda x:x[0]) # lambda not allowed! Use `top.sort()` since tuples. No lambda.
       if len(top)>12: top.pop()
    if not np.isfinite(old) or score < old - 1e-5:
       print(...)
```

`normalize_nonneg`:
```
def normalize_nonneg(a):
    a=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
    if a.size==0: return np.ones(1)
    a=np.clip(a,0.0,None)
    s=a.sum()
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-300:
       a=np.ones_like(a); s=a.size
    return a/s
```

   if n>1024: return None

   return g
```

### Local loop detailed code

```
def local_loop(a, time_limit, state, rng):
    a = normalize_nonneg(a)
    n = a.size
    deadline = time.monotonic() + max(0.0, time_limit)
    c = fft_conv(a); M=max; best_score=2nM; best=a.copy(); update.
    no_improve=0; temp_frac=0.15; it=0
    last_lp = 0.0; lp_time_spent=0.0
    use_lp = linprog is not None and n <= 768
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
       it +=1
       c=fft_conv(a); M=float(c.max()); cur=2*n*M
       if cur < best_score - 1e-12:
          best_score=cur; best=a.copy(); no_improve=0; temp_frac=max(0.02, temp_frac*0.96); update_global(state,best,cur)
       else:
          no_improve +=1
       if no_improve > 45:
          temp_frac = min(0.8, temp_frac*1.35)
          a = random_perturb(a, rng, 0.25)
          continue
       moved=False
       now = time.monotonic()
       if use_lp and (it % 7 == 0) and (lp_time_spent < 0.35*time_limit or time_limit>300):
           pref = 'tan' if (it % 21 == 0) else 'sot'
           t0=time.monotonic()
           try:
              g = solve_lp_sot(a,M,choose_rows(c, min(2*n-1, 250)))
           except Exception: g=None
           if g is None and pref=='tan': ...
           lp_time_spent += time.monotonic()-t0
           if g is not None:
               h,sc = line_search_mix(a,g,cur)
               if sc < cur - 1e-12:
                  a=h; moved=True
       if not moved and time.monotonic() < deadline:
           h = smooth_step(a,c,M,temp_frac,cur)
           if h is not None:
               a=h; moved=True
       if not moved:
           a = random_perturb(a, rng, 0.07)
    # exact final
    scx = score_exact_limit(best, n<=4096)
    if scx < best_score: best... update
    return best,best_score
```

`line_search_mix`:
```
def line_search_mix(a,g,cur):
   g=normalize_nonneg(g); best=a; bestcur=cur
   for t in (0.05,0.15,0.3,0.5,0.75,1.0):
       h=normalize_nonneg((1-t)*a+t*g)
       sc=score_fast(h)
       if sc<bestcur: best=h; bestcur=sc
   return best,bestcur
```

def score_positive(a):
   n=a.size

   c=fft_conv(a); M=float(c.max()); return 2.0*n*M
```

### L-BFGS function detailed

```
def run_lbfbs(a, time_limit, state, rng=None):
    if minimize is None: return a
    a=normalize_nonneg(a); n=a.size
    if n > 2500: # too slow maybe skip
        # maybe downsample to 1024? no, just return
        return a
    deadline = time.monotonic() + max(0.0, time_limit)
    best=a.copy(); best_score=score_positive(a); update_global...
    fracs = [0.45, 0.25, 0.12, 0.06, 0.03, 0.015, 0.008, 0.004]
    frac_idx=0; no_improve=0; cycle_no=0
    while time.monotonic() > deadline - 25:
        frac = fracs[frac_idx % len(fracs)]
        if no_improve > 3 and frac < 0.1: frac = 0.25
        M = best_score / (2.0*n)
        T = max(1e-12, frac * M)
        ctx = {'T': float(T), 'state': state, 'best_score': [best_score], 'best_w': None}
        global _CTX
        _CTX = ctx
        w = np.log(best + 1e-15); w -= w.mean()
        _CTX_extra? _STOP_DEADLINE = deadline - 10
        # choose maxiter
        maxiter = 60 if n > 1024 else 120
        try:
            res = minimize(_objective_global, w, jac=_gradient_global, method='L-BFGS-B',
                           callback=_minimize_callback,
                           options={'maxiter': maxiter, 'ftol': 1e-13, 'gtol': 1e-8, 'maxls': 20, 'disp': False})
        except Exception:
            res = None
        # retrieve best
        if ctx['best_w'] is not None:
            w_best = ctx['best_w']
        elif res is not None and res.x is not None:
            w_best = res.x
        else:
            w_best = w
        a_new = softmax_from_log(w_best)
        sc = score_positive(a_new)
        if sc < best_score - 1e-12:
            best = a_new; best_score = sc; no_improve = 0; frac_idx += 1
            update_global(state,best,best_score)
        else:
            no_improve += 1
            # maybe try bigger T already; also perturb
            if no_improve > 2:
                best = random_perturb(best, np.random.default_rng(int(time.time()*1000)), 0.15) if rng is None else random_perturb(best, rng, 0.15)
                best_score = score_positive(best)
                no_improve = 0
                frac_idx = 0
            else:
                frac_idx = (frac_idx + 1) % len(fracs)
    # final exact
    scx=score_exact_limit(best)
    if scx < best_score: best... update
    return best
```

Need if rng None create? Passing rng from propose to avoid creating many. Use `random_perturb(best, rng, 0.15)`. Ensure rng defined.

The objective global updates state and ctx best. It may update state with a sequence from a different length? In `run_lbfbs`, only current. Good. But if objective updates global best with score lower, state global seq changes; local best also. At end, if `state['seq']` from another trajectory with lower score? Since single-thread and state global, if current run updates, okay. If an earlier candidate in state top has lower score but not in this run, local `best` may not be global. At final output use state top; local return not critical. But in `run_lbfbs`, if state has global best lower than local before start, ctx best_score also? We set ctx best_score = current local, not state. Objective may find score between state and local; update global? `update_global` compares global. If local not global, still okay. But if state global is lower, this run cannot improve below local unless finds better. Good.

Potential issue: `_objective_global` updates state seq to `a` (cut vector) whenever score below global. If during L-BFGS line search it evaluates a temporary point with lower score than global but later not final, state updates. That's okay (we keep best valid). Top stores it.

Potential issue: `_objective_global` uses `fft_conv` which for n=2048 maybe slower; L-BFGS many calls. Limit n <=1024 for L-BFGS? The score may improve at 1024; n=2048 maybe too slow. Set max n for L-BFGS 1536. For n=2048, use gradient loop? Hmm. We can run L-BFGS at 1024. Larger n maybe just local_loop (FFT gradient) not LP. n=2048 FFT fine, gradient loop okay. ### `score_exact_limit`

```
def score_exact_limit(a, max_n=4096):
   a=normalize_nonneg(a); n=a.size
   if n<=max_n:
      s=a.sum(); if s<... inf
      c=np.convolve(a,a); M=float(c.max()); return 2*n*M/(s*s)
   else:
      return score_positive(a)
```

```
def add_two_block_candidates(N, state, max_evals=80):
   best=None
   len_fracs = np.linspace(0.15,0.45,12)
   mass_vals = np.linspace(0.15,0.85,12)
   for lf in len_fracs:
      L=int(max(2, round(lf*N)))
      if L>=N: continue
      for p in mass_vals:
         h=np.zeros(N); h[:L]=p/L; h[L:]=(1-p)/(N-L)
         sc=score_positive(h)
         update_global(state,h,sc)
```
This is 144 evals each O(N^2) maybe for N=1024 144M, okay maybe few seconds? Use FFT score_positive; 144*FFT 1024 ~ okay. Could reduce to 5x5=25. Do N=512,1024. Not critical.

Propose function pseudocode:
```
def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=1800):
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    hard_deadline = time.monotonic() + max(0.0, float(budget_s)-60)
    state = {'score':inf,'seq':None,'top':[]}
    provided = load_provided_sequence()
    if provided is not None: update_global(state, provided, score_positive(provided))
    # fallbacks
    update_global(state, np.array([1/3,2/3]), score_positive(...))
    add_two_block_candidates(256, state)
    add_two_block_candidates(512, state)
    # If provided exists and size reasonable, use as start
    if state['seq'] is None: seq = make_initial(512,5,rng)
    else: seq = state['seq']
    # If provided length not 512? maybe use its length.
    n0 = state['seq'].size
    if n0 < 256: start_seq = resize_to_length(state['seq'],512); else if n0>1024: start_seq=resize_to_length(state['seq'],512) maybe.
    # main refine list
    tasks = []
    # primary: if n0 around 512, refine at 512
    tasks.append((resize_to_length(state['seq'],512), 540.0))
    tasks.append((resize_to_length(state['seq'],1024), 480.0))
    tasks.append((resize_to_length(state['seq'],2048), 240.0))
    # diverse at 512
    for kind in (5,4,0):
       tasks.append((make_initial(512,kind,rng,state['seq'],provided), 180.0))
    for seq,t in tasks:
       if time.monotonic()+30 > hard_deadline: break
       # adaptive time: if no time, skip
       limit = min(t, hard_deadline - time.monotonic())
       if limit < 30: continue
       # If seq internal score much worse than state and n large, reduce time? maybe still
       seq = special_refine(seq, limit, state, rng)
    # final exact evaluate top
    final_seq = None; final_off=inf
    for sc,seq in state['top'][:12]:
       # maybe exact official
       cand=[float(x) for x in normalize_nonneg(seq)]
       try: off=evaluate_sequence(cand)
       except: off=inf
       if off < final_off: final_off=off; final_seq=cand
    if final_seq is None: final_seq=[float(x) for x in state['seq']]
    return final_seq
```

def special_refine(a, time_limit, state, rng):
   a=normalize_nonneg(a); n=a.size
   deadline=now+time_limit
   # If L-BFGS available and enough time
   lbfbs_time = min(0.5*time_limit, max(30, 300 if n<=512 else 150))
   if lbfbs_time>30 and minimize:
       a=run_lbfbs(a, lbfbs_time, state, rng)
   rem=deadline-time.monotonic()
   if rem>30:
       a=local_loop(a, rem, state, rng)
   return a
```

### Code details for `_objective_global`

```
def _objective_global(x):
    ctx = _CTX
    if ctx is None:
        return 0.0
    w = np.asarray(x, dtype=np.float64)
    w = w - np.max(w)
    e = np.exp(w)
    s = e.sum()
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0:
        return 1e100
    a = e / s
    c = fft_conv(a)
    M = float(c.max())
    T = float(ctx['T'])
    score = 2.0 * a.size * M
    best_arr = ctx.get('best_score')
    if best_arr is not None and score < best_arr[0] - 1e-12:
        best_arr[0] = float(score)
        ctx['best_w'] = x.copy()
        update_global(ctx['state'], a, float(score))
    if T <= 0:
        return M
    # logsumexp stable
    svals = c - M
    # To avoid overflow, svals/T <=0, but if T tiny, exp underflow okay
    sumexp = float(np.sum(np.exp(svals / T)))
    if not np.isfinite(sumexp) or sumexp <= 0.0:
        # fallback: smooth less
        return M
    val = 0.5 * M + 0.5 * T * float(np.log(sumexp))
    if not np.isfinite(val):
        return M
    return float(val)
```
If `x` is large length, `x.copy()` okay. We assume x length n.

Gradient:
```
def _gradient_global(x):
  ctx=_CTX; if None return zeros
  w=...
  a=...
  c=fft_conv(a); M=max; T=ctx['T']
  s=c-M
  p=np.exp(s/T); ps=p.sum(); if ps<=0: p=zeros; p[argmax]=1 else p/=ps
  r=cross_pow(p,a)
  # ensure finite
  mean_wr = float(np.dot(a, r))
  grad = a * (r - mean_wr)
  # remove nan
  if not np.all(np.isfinite(grad)): grad = np.zeros_like(a)
  return grad
```
Derivative of F_scaled = 0.5 logsumexp? Actually we set objective val = 0.5M+0.5T logS = (T/2) logsumexp(c/T). Gradient wrt w = a*(r - mean_wr). Check factor? Let F=(T/2)logsumexp(c/T). ∂F/∂a = 0.5 ∑p∂c = r. Then grad_w = a*(r - a·r). Yes.

If T large, p nearly uniform; r maybe mean small.

def update_global(...):
   top = state.setdefault('top', [])
   if len(top) < 12:
      top.append((score, seq.copy()))
   else:

          if top[i][0] > worst_val:
              worst_val=top[i][0]; worst_idx=i

```

### `special_refine`:
```
def special_refine(a, time_limit, state, rng):
    a=normalize_nonneg(a)
    if state['seq'] is not None:
       sc_state=state['score']; sc_a=score_positive(a)
       if sc_state < sc_a - 1e-12:
           a=state['seq'].copy()
    if time_limit <=0: return a
    deadline=now+time_limit
    # L-BFGS phase
    if minimize is not None and a.size <= 2048:
       l_time = min(0.55*time_limit, max(60.0, (300.0 if a.size <=512 else 150.0)))
       l_time = min(l_time, max(0.0, deadline-workingnow-30))
       if l_time > 30:
          a = run_lbfbs(a, l_time, state, rng)
    # local loop
    rem = deadline - time.monotonic()
    if rem > 30:
       a = local_loop(a, rem, state, rng)
    return a
```
If L-BFGS phase ends with state global better than a? `run_lbfbs` returns local best. It may not return global if global from objective? It updates local at end from ctx, but if state global better? At start of local loop, we can again choose state seq if better.

Could there be a more powerful method: **MultiplicativeWeiszfeld**: At current a, p_k weights on active conv, the gradient r_j =∑p_k a_{k-j}. Multiplicative update a_j <- a_j / r_j? This is like convex optimization for l1? Let's derive if minimizing ∑ c_k with constraints? Not. But we can use **iterative proportional fitting** to make convolution as flat as possible: Given target c, update a to reduce discrepancy. The condition for optimal c flat? KKT: there exist λ_k≥0 such that 2∑ λ_k a_{k-j} = μ + ν_j (ν_j≥0 for nonneg). This is exactly gradient. The multiplicative update `a_j <- a_j / (∑ λ_k a_{k-j})` might converge to a point? This is the **Sinkhorn** for tensor decomposition. We can try: normalize weights p (KKT dual). Update a_new = normalize(a / r) where r = cross(p,a). This tends to equalize weighted contributions. Could be effective. Let's include in smooth_step candidate:
- `h = normalize_nonneg(a / np.maximum(r, 1e-300))`? If r small, huge. Use log: `h = normalize(exp(log(a+eps) - log(r+eps)))`. This is natural gradient with large step? Since grad_w = a*(r-mean), for large eta, a*exp(-eta a*(r-mean)) not this. Multiplicative by 1/r is different (like minorization). Try step `h = normalize_nonneg(a / (1 + alpha*r))`? ### Let's ensure `random_perturb` uses `rng` and returns normalized. For high zero, add floor.
```
def random_perturb(a, rng, sigma=0.1):
    a = np.asarray(a,float).copy()
    n=a.size
    if n == 1: return normalize_nonneg(a)
    base = np.maximum(a, 1e-15)
    base *= np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, sigma, size=n))
    w1 = max(1, n//200)
    w2 = max(w1+1, n//8)
    width = int(rng.integers(w1, w2+1))
    start = int(rng.integers(0, max(1, n-width+1)))
    base[start:start+width] *= np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, 1.5*sigma, size=width))
    return normalize_nonneg(base)
```
If n small, w1=1,w2=1 width1.

### `make_initial` details:
```
def make_initial(N, kind, rng, best_seq=None, provided_seq=None):
    N=int(N)
    if kind == -1 and best_seq is not None: return resize_to_length(best_seq,N)
    if kind == -2 and provided_seq is not None: return resize_to_length(provided_seq,N)
    x=np.linspace(0,1,N)
    if kind==0:
       mu=rng.uniform(0.2,0.8); sig=rng.uniform(0.1,0.4); a=np.exp(-0.5*((x-mu)/sig)**2)
    elif kind==1:
       alpha=rng.choice([-3,-2,-1,1,2,3]); a=np.exp(alpha*x)
    elif kind==2:
       alpha=rng.uniform(0,2); beta=rng.uniform(0,2); xs=linspace(...); a=xs**alpha*(1-xs)**beta
    elif kind==3:
       L=int(rng.integers(2,max(3,N//2)))
       p=rng.uniform(0.1,0.9); h=zeros; h[:L]=p/L; h[L:]=(1-p)/(N-L)
    elif kind==4:
       K=int(rng.integers(2,6)); breaks=sort; masses=dirichlet; h blocks
    elif kind==5:
       base=rng.normal(0,1,N); wlen=max(1,N//20); smooth; a=np.exp(0.5*base)
    elif kind==6:
       b=rng.uniform(1,4); a=np.exp(-b*x) # not normalized yet
    elif kind==7:
       # three block fixed ratios maybe
       L1=int(N*0.2); L2=int(N*0.5); ...
       a zeros, masses热门?
    else: a=rng.random(N)
    return normalize_nonneg(np.maximum(a,0))
```
Need if N-L zero; ensure L<N.

lengths = (N * lengths).astype(int)
lengths = np.maximum(1, lengths)
diff = N - lengths.sum()
while diff >0: lengths[rng.integers(K)] +=1; diff-=1
while diff<0:
   idx = int(np.argmax(lengths)); if lengths[idx]>1: lengths[idx]-=1; diff+=1 else break
h=zeros; pos=0; for L,m in zip(lengths,masses): if L>0: h[pos:pos+L]=m/L; pos+=L

### Let's double-check L-BFGS gradient consistency with softmax and objective one more time.

Objective: F(w) = (T/2) L(a(w)), L(a)=logsumexp(c/T)? Actually F = T/2 * log∑ exp(c_k/T). We compute val = 0.5 M + 0.5 T logS where S=∑ exp((c-M)/T). This equals T/2*(M/T + logS)=T/2 log∑ exp(c/T). Yes. Derivative: ∇F(a) = T/2 * (1/T) ∑ exp(c/T)/∑ exp(c/T) * ∇c_k = 0.5 ∑ p ∇c_k. ∇c_k(a) = 2 r_k? Vector r_j =∑ p_k? Let's define vector gradient g_a_j=0.5 ∑_k p_k 2 a_{k-j} = r_j. Good. ∇w_i = ∑_j g_a_j ∂a_j/∂w_i = a_i(g_a_i - ∑_j a_j g_a_j). ∑ a_j g_a_j = ∑ a_j r_j. So grad = a*(r - dot(a,r)). Good.

def fft_cross_conv(a,b):
   n=a.size; m=b.size; total=n+m-1; size power2; fa[:n]=a; fb[:m]=b; out=irfft(rfft(fa)*rfft(fb)); return out[:total]
```

Let's write `downsample(a,N)` robust:
```
def downsample(a,N):
    m=a.size
    if N<=1: return np.ones((1,), dtype=float)
    if m<=N: return upsample(a,N)
    out=np.zeros(N)
    for i in range(N):
        lo=(i*m)//N
        hi=((i+1)*m)//N
        if hi>lo: out[i]=a[lo:hi].sum()
    return normalize_nonneg(out)
```

`smooth_step` uses `fft_conv`, `cross_pow`, `score_positive`, `normalize_nonneg`.

Implement `smooth_step`:
```
def smooth_step(a,c,M,temp_frac,cur_score):
   if M<=0: return None
   T=max(1e-15, temp_frac*M)
   s=(c-M)/T
   s=np.clip(s,-60.0,0.0)
   p=np.exp(s); ps=p.sum()
   if ps<=0: p=zeros; p[int(np.argmax(c))]=1 else p/=ps
   r=cross_pow(p,a); r=np.where(np.isfinite(r),r,0)
   best=a; best_cur=cur_score
   # additive backtracking
   d=r - np.mean(r)
   scale=float(np.max(np.abs(d)))
   if scale>0:
      alpha=1.0
      for _ in range(10):
         h=normalize_nonneg(a - alpha*scale*d)
         sc=score_positive(h)
         if sc < best_cur - 1e-15:
            best=h; best_cur=sc
            break
         alpha *=0.5
   # multiplicative natural
   dn = a*(r - float(np.dot(a,r)))
   scale_n=float(np.max(np.abs(dn)))
   if scale_n>0:
      alpha=1.0
      for _ in range(10):
         h=normalize_nonneg(a * np.exp(-alpha*scale_n*dn))
         sc=score_positive(h)
         if sc<best_cur...:
         alpha*=0.5
   if best_cur < cur_score - 1e-15: return best
   return None
```
Potential: `a - alpha*scale*d` may become negative; normalize clips. If all zero? No.

`line_search_mix` similar.

`local_loop` uses these. Ensure if `a` becomes all zero? normalize returns ones; score maybe 2; state avoids.

### Let's verify `_gradient_global` returns zero if ctx T invalid. If minimize called with `jac` and returns non-finite, may fail. Ensure finite.
